Podzolization, Lateritic Leaching & Intrazonal Soils

  • Earthworms contribute to the formation of the soil and also increase its productivity. Hence, they are known as engineers of the ecosystem. Earthworms create pores in the soil, allowing the percolation of air and water and release of carbon dioxide.
  • The excreted urea and dead matter of living organisms (flora and fauna) are decomposed by decomposers in the soil.
  • Soil profile is a vertical section of soil in which all the soil horizons are shown. They are as follows: (1) Eluviations – The matter of upper stratum descends downwards. (2) Illuviation – Different salts are introduced from the upper horizon to lower due to percolation of water. (3) Capillary Action – It is responsible for upward movement of water from wet areas to dry areas of the soil. The smaller the pore the higher the capillary action and vice versa.
  • चेरनोज़म मृदा [Chernozem Black Soil] soil is found in grassland and transitional areas. It is mostly found in a temperate steppe climate and is also found in humid grasslands.
  • Chestnut soil is found in dry areas. It is dark brown in color and has a low humus content compared to चेरनोज़म मृदा [Chernozem Black Soil] soil.
  • Terra Rossa soil is a reddish silty soil, formed as a result of weathering of limestones and dolomites.

Chapter 82

Mapping

Reviews map projections, cartographic scale principles, and isopleth isolines connecting points of equal value. Tests Mercator projection distortion, topographic contour lines, isobars, isotherms, isohyets, and isohalines.

UPPSC asks most here

  1. An imaginary line or a line on a map joining points on the Earth’s surface at which the magnetic declination is the same called...Lower Sub ’981 paper
  2. Isopleths are contour lines connecting geographical locations having equal numerical values of a variable (temperature, pressure, rain).PCS Pre ’121 paper
  3. Isobars connect points of equal atmospheric pressure, while Isotherms connect points experiencing equal mean temperatures.PCS Pre ’071 paper

Cartographic Principles, Map Projections & Scales

  • Maps provide us with relevant information regarding regional, territorial and local changes.
  • Contours represent elevation topography in comparison to the sea level. It is the most practical and widely used method in showing elevations on a map. When the contours of Isotypes are drawn very close to each other then it signifies steep slopes. Isohypse - isohypse or height contour is a line of equal geopotential height, which are shown on a constant pressure surface. Isogonic lines show the earth’s magnetic declination on a map. Isobaths are imaginary lines, a map that links points at the same depth below the water surface.
  • Isohyets are lines on the map joining areas having an equal amount of rainfall.
  • Isohalines are lines on the map joining areas having an equal amount of salinity.
  • An imaginary line or a line on a map joining points on the Earth’s surface at which the magnetic declination is the same called, isogonal. UPPSC

Isopleth Isolines & Contours (Isobars, Isotherms, Isohyets, Isohalines)

  • Isopleth maps provide us information about regions or areas having continuous distribution. Thus isopleths are lines joining places of equal value of quantity, intensity, equal pressure, equal temperature, equal rainfall, equal salinity etc. A very relevant and precise data is required for drawing the lines of isopleths. If there is equitable distribution of the given subject then isopleths become very useful. Moreover, the use of isopleths becomes difficult if the subject is in constant fluctuation for exp. population density.
  • Isobars are imaginary lines drawn on the maps that links areas having equal air pressure at mean sea level.
  • Isonifs are lines on the map joining areas having an equal amount of snowfall.
  • Ferdinand Magellan was a Portuguese explorer and the first person to circumnavigate the world.
  • Isopleths are contour lines connecting geographical locations having equal numerical values of a variable (temperature, pressure, rain). UPPSC
  • Isobars connect points of equal atmospheric pressure, while Isotherms connect points experiencing equal mean temperatures. UPPSC
  • Isohyets are isolines connecting places receiving equal amounts of rainfall during a specified duration. UPPSC
  • Isohalines connect points of equal salinity in oceans and enclosed seas, indicating salinity gradients. UPPSC
  • समोच्च रेखाएं [Contour Lines] connect points of equal elevation above sea level; closely spaced contours indicate steep topography. UPPSC

Maritime Anomalies & Epic Continental Shelves (Bermuda, Grand Banks)

  • Contours are an effective device for representing relief on topographic maps. They can be defined as an imaginary line connecting points of equal elevation on the ground surface. When contours are close together, they represent a steep slope, whereas when contours are far from each other, they represent a gradual slope. 6. The line joining the areas having the same rainfall is called : (a) समदाब रेखा [Isobar] (b) समवर्षा रेखा [Isohyet] (c) समताप रेखा [Isotherm] (d) समलवण रेखा [Isohaline] M.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2015 Ans. (b) The line drawn on the map joining the same amount of rain- fall is called an Isohyet. An Isobar or Isobaric line is a line drawn on the map joining the places of same atmospheric pressure at mean sea level. An Isotherm is a line drawn on a map joining places of same temperature at mean sea level. Isohaline is a line on a map joining places of same salinity in the oceans. Miscellaneous
